**11-20 is closed: SHELL-04 holds in code, and the one thing it cannot prove is named rather than glossed.** Three `opengraph-image.tsx` where zero existed — a root card, a listing card (title, `{Space type} · {City}`, the all-in RATE, never a computed total), and a **CONSTANT** invite card. The invite image takes **no argument at all** rather than an ignored one, imports nothing but the token table and the shared renderer, and returns **byte-identical bytes** (sha256 `1603284e7087bd70`, 12,781 B) for a valid, an unknown, a cancelled-booking, a malformed and a whitespace token against a production `next start`. The reasoning that made that the right design is in the file: the PAGE may differ between a live invite and an inactive one (showing the event is the product) and 08-06's property — unknown = revoked = voided = cancelled = malformed, one indistinguishable state — is preserved exactly, with all three inactive causes emitting the identical title and description; the IMAGE is a **new** unauthenticated endpoint that chat services, scanners and spam filters fetch automatically, so it may not vary at all. **THREE Next-16 landmines, measured, two of them walked into by the plan's own instructions.** (1) The DOCUMENTED font load — `fetch(new URL("./X.ttf", import.meta.url))` — does not work on the Node runtime: `new URL` resolves to `file://`, undici does not implement that scheme, and it rejects with `fetch failed`. Without the fallback rung already written, every card in the app would have shipped in `next/og`'s bundled face at weight 400, silently, at a 200. Fixed with `fs.readFile(fileURLToPath(url))`, keeping the `new URL` so Turbopack still emits and hashes the asset; verified in dev **and** against `next start`, where the production log carries **zero** `[og]` fallback lines. (2) **Declaring `openGraph` at all DELETES the file-convention `og:image`** and downgrades `twitter:card` from `summary_large_image` to `summary` — measured both ways on one route with nothing else changed, so the edit that adds tags is the edit that removes the card and it looks *more* complete afterwards. (3) The invite card is served at `/invite/<token>/opengraph-image-ikagei`; the unsuffixed path is a **404**. `getMetadataRouteSuffix` appends a 6-char djb2 hash of the parent path whenever any parent segment is a route group — so plan 11-10's `(public)` is what puts it there, and the plan's own acceptance criterion spells an unsatisfiable URL. **`new ImageResponse(...)` cannot fail safely by itself** — it extends `Response` and runs satori inside the body stream's `start()`, with the status line already committed, so a `try`/`catch` around it catches nothing; `renderOgCard` awaits `.arrayBuffer()` and returns fresh bytes. Three rungs, **all three watched firing**: a runtime font-read failure → 200 / 25,149 B / 1200×630 legible on both routes, and a forced rung-2 failure → 200 / 70 B / a real 1×1 PNG. The hole is stated too: a *syntax error* in the module is still a 500 (found by accident — a `**/` inside a block comment closed it early), and only `npm run build` catches that. **The conversion also fixed a shipped bug**: the invite title was hand-written before `layout.tsx:107` declared `template: "%s · FitOut"` and rendered `You're invited · FitOut · FitOut` on the app's only fully public link-shared page. `tests/design/og-routes.test.ts` carries AC#12/#13/#14 over the AST with an **allow-list** rather than the prescribed denylist (a denylist over `@/lib/db` is walked around by `../../../lib/db`), content closure stated forward plus a count, and **six watched reds** — including (d) vacuity, which turns 6 of 12 red with the guard first, and (e1)/(e2) paired because (e1) alone stays green **on purpose** and would have been another vacuous probe. <details><summary>Previous status (plan 11-10, superseded 2026-08-14 — note that 11-11 and 11-12 shipped without adding a status paragraph here; their SUMMARY files are the record)</summary>

**11-10 is closed: SHELL-01's four navigation-less routes have a header, and the checkout route has the same box with nothing in it to click.** `patterns/site-chrome.tsx` owns GEOMETRY ONLY — `HEADER_HEIGHT`, `sticky top-0 z-(--z-sticky) border-b`, a `max-w-6xl` container and three named slots (`brand`, `nav`, `actions`) — so D-04 survives verbatim: what is shared is the BOX, not the wordmark, the surface or the link set, and the two in-file *"Do NOT refactor the two headers into one here"* instructions are honoured (plan 11-12 amends them). **The headline result is measured, not asserted:** in a real browser at 320/375/640/768/1280 in **court AND grove**, the header is `56, 56, 64, 64, 64` — the same five numbers in both themes, no horizontal overflow at 320px in either. Both shipped rails moved `lg:top-8` → `lg:top-20`: "minimal" is about what the checkout header CONTAINS, not how tall it is, so its rail tucked under exactly as the listing page's did. `tests/design/sticky-offset.test.ts` asserts the RULE (every `lg:sticky` offset ≥ 80px) over an **AST paired scan** rather than a grep for one wrong value — the prescribed `grep "lg:top-8"` is green on a broken tree because it counts prose and because `lg:top-4`/`lg:top-12`/`lg:top-[60px]` are all equally wrong and invisible to it. Ten deviations, three of them real bugs: the second sticky rail, `panel-card.tsx`'s "both offset by 32px" note going false, and two "do not move this under a route group" comments made false by the move (all amended, never deleted, each preserving the load-bearing half — *no ancestor layout may read a session and redirect*). `site-brand` is written as a literal JSX attribute in BOTH `brandHref` branches on purpose: the tidier spread and named-constant forms are invisible to `selector-contract.test.ts`'s literal-attribute walk, and blinding the gate is a worse trade than a repeated literal (DOM truth measured — exactly one node per branch, `A` vs `SPAN`, byte-identical class). Two pinned inventories moved, both watched red first: `STICKY_INVENTORY` 12→13 (**permanent**, unlike row-card's) and `DISPLAY_INVENTORY`'s two path keys renamed. `measurements.ts` gained its 8th and 9th constants because `auth-slot-skeleton.tsx` matches `patterns/*skeleton*.tsx` and AC#16 bans the literals the UI-SPEC prescribes for it. Half 1 was already immune without any stripping. Skipping that probe — or rationalising its green — would have shipped a WATCHED RED section describing a mutation that never failed, which is 11-02's finding in a new disguise. Two fixes rather than one: a direct assertion on `ruleLines()` (Rule 2) makes the stripper's **real** job load-bearing — this `.gitignore` holds **50 non-blank lines, 17 prose, 33 rules**, so an unstripped floor of 20 would pass against a file gutted down to its own commentary — and a **fourth** probe (`hasRule` reduced to a substring check) covers the failure mode the fixture was actually written for. All four results are verbatim in the gate's header, **including the green one**. `tests/design/gitignore-baselines.test.ts` is 4 assertions, DB-free, inside `npm run build`, and is **the repo's first test to shell out to `git`**: half 2 asks `git ls-files` rather than walking the filesystem, because an untracked `*-win32.png` is harmless and expected (it is what a local `--update-snapshots` produces) and only a **committed** one is the defect — and because `.gitignore` is *advisory*, so `git add -f` bypasses every rule half 1 asserts. A failed `git` invocation is a **named assertion failure**, never a vacuous pass, and two control pathspecs prove the query shape still matches — one of them crossing a directory boundary, since a real baseline sits four levels deep. Probe (b) staged exactly such a file and **also corrected the gate's own remedy sentence**: `git rm --cached` does NOT delete the working-tree copy (measured — the PNG stayed on disk and `git status --ignored` then reported `!! e2e/visual/`, the harmless end state). <details><summary>Previous status (plan 11-02, superseded 2026-08-13)</summary>

**11-02 (GATE-04's regression-blocking half) is closed, and the plan's own probe design had a hole its author could not have seen without running it.** `src/lib/design/selector-contract.ts` declares all **17** structural hooks this milestone may render as a const tuple → derived union → **total `Record`** (D-31, superseding D-134's `SELECTOR-CONTRACT.md` name), every row carrying a mandatory `why` and an `owner` naming the plan that ships it. The compile gate was **watched**: deleting the `panel-card` row gave `tsc` exit 2 and `TS2741: Property '"panel-card"' is missing…`, recorded verbatim — and the *second* useful half of that output is that the required type prints the union **member by member** rather than as the alias, so the missing id is legible on both sides of the error. `tests/design/selector-contract.test.ts` is 5 assertions, DB-free, inside `npm run build`: the **D-32 floor** (`getByRole >= 92`, `getByLabel >= 30`, floors and never equalities, because an equality at 92/30 goes red on every legitimate new assertion Phases 12-19 write and the fix for that red is to bump the number — the rubber-stamp reflex arriving through the gate meant to prevent it), the **undeclared-id ban** (AST scan of all 128 `src/**/*.tsx`, every `data-testid` string literal must be in `SELECTOR_IDS`), and guard-the-guard over **both** trees. **THE HEADLINE FINDING IS THAT A GUARD-THE-GUARD PROBE CAN ITSELF BE VACUOUS, AND THE PLAN'S THREE PROBES WERE.** The plan prescribed breaking `E2E_DIR`; that gives **2 failed / 3 passed** because `0 >= 92` is also false, and a reader concludes the floor covers the vacuity case — exactly backwards. A **fourth** probe was added (Rule 2) breaking `SRC_DIR` instead: **1 failed / 4 passed**, and **the undeclared-id ban PASSED**, reporting a perfectly clean `[]` against a tree it never opened, indistinguishable from a real clean run and green forever. **An absence assertion cannot notice it was handed nothing** — only a positive control over its own scan can, which is why `tsxFiles.length >= 50` now sits beside `specFiles.length >= 10` and why `collectFiles()` returns `[]` rather than throwing (a throw is caught by whoever moved the directory; the realistic failure is a scan narrowed by a wrong glob, which never throws at all). All four REDs are verbatim in the gate's header, including the floor's, where **`.locator(` moved 23 → 24 in the same failure message that showed `getByRole` moving 92 → 91** — which is why the three ungated counts are printed inside the gated assertion rather than buried in a comment. **The measured counts are 92 / 30 / 63 / 23 and they are identical raw and comment-stripped today** — no spec quotes a query token in prose yet, so the shared `stripComments` changes nothing at HEAD and is there for the day a well-meant comment explaining a `getByRole` choice inflates the floor. Only `getByRole` and `getByLabel` are gated: a `getByText` floor would freeze copy this phase is about to rewrite, and a `.locator(` floor would be perverse — those 23 structural calls ARE the fragile selectors the phase exists to reduce. **`11-UI-SPEC.md` § GATE-04 says 22; the tree holds 23 and the measurement wins.** Two further plan-vs-tree corrections: the prescribed floor probe names a `getByRole("link", …)` in `public-listing.spec.ts` that does not exist (the file holds exactly two, at `:107` and `:112`; `:112` was used), and the phase's standing `ls drizzle/ | tail -1` check returns **`meta`**, not `0025_audit_resolved_by.sql`, because `ls` sorts the directory last — later plans should use `ls drizzle/*.sql | tail -1`. **GATE-04 stays Pending on purpose**: three plans claim it, and `11-05` (the (N+1)th-booking mutation proof) and `11-22` (the forward direction — "every declared id actually appears in `src/`") own the other two clauses. That hand-off is written into the gate's NOT COVERED block **and** made auditable by the `owner` column, because at wave 1 the forward assertion is vacuous against an empty set and from wave 4 it is red for every id whose owning plan has not run. <details><summary>Previous status (plan 11-01, superseded 2026-08-13)</summary>

**11-01 (GATE-05) is closed, and the plan's own `must_haves` contained a claim the tree cannot produce.** `import "server-only"` now guards eight money/availability computation modules, and `npm run build` hard-fails on any client graph that reaches them (D-34) — with **zero packages installed**, because Next aliases the specifier itself. Two splits made that possible without false positives: the three money rates left `payments/config.ts` for a new guarded `payments/fees.ts` (leaving the 14 timing constants `slot-picker.tsx:43` and `request-row.tsx:34` legitimately import), and `BOOKING_HORIZON_DAYS` left `availability/slots.ts` for a new unguarded `availability/horizon.ts`. Both live D-130 violations are fixed and **`SERVICE_FEE_BPS` is provably absent from every emitted client chunk — 5 chunks at HEAD, 0 now**. **THE HEADLINE FINDING IS THAT THE BUILD IS A LOWER BOUND, NOT A LIST.** The plan expected ONE failing build naming `listing-card.tsx` AND `availability-calendar.tsx`; that build does not exist. Turbopack prints **one import trace per (module, environment)**, so RED #1 (6 errors) named `listing-card.tsx` in every client trace and never mentioned `availability-calendar.tsx` — whose guard was already in place and whose `computeServiceFee` import was untouched. Only after the first was repaired did RED #2 (4 errors) name the second. Adopting `server-only` on a multi-offender tree is a **fix-and-rebuild loop**, and a plan reading run #1 as complete ships the rest; what closes it is the emitted-chunk grep, a count over the whole output rather than a first-match trace. Both REDs are committed verbatim in the gate's header, with the sentence naming what did NOT fail — `slot-picker.tsx` and `request-row.tsx` are absent from every diagnostic, and no diagnostic mentions `slots.ts`/`read-model.ts`/`units.ts` at all, which is what makes the two splits correct rather than lucky. **The props-contract decision (RESEARCH Open Question 3) went to a TABLE, not a unit rate, on a measured ground:** `computeServiceFee` rounds ONCE over the whole space price, so `n × allIn(unit) ≠ allIn(n × unit)` — at the seeded ₱307.50 rate the multiply runs **ahead** of the frozen quote, bounded by n−1 centavos, which is D-75's forbidden direction. Both rail call sites' own comments call their figure *exact*; a multiply would have made those sentences false while every gate stayed green. That argument is now **executable** (`tests/booking/all-in-table.test.ts` case 2) rather than prose. `ListingCardData` **lost its five rate columns entirely** rather than keeping them unused — the inputs are structurally absent from the client, so the arithmetic cannot return without a deliberate props change. Gate: `tests/design/server-only-guards.test.ts`, 7 assertions, DB-free, inside `npm run build`, **watched red THREE ways** (guard deleted → 1/5 naming that file; guard ADDED to `config.ts` → 1/5 on a *different* assertion; transitivity broken by inlining the formula → 1/6 — and that third is the one **Turbopack itself goes quiet on**, because nothing guarded is reached any more). **Grep is banned in that file for a measured reason:** `grep -rn "server-only" src/` returned 6 hits across 5 files at HEAD and **every one was a comment**. The same collision recurred at verification time — grep still finds `computeServiceFee` 4× and `allInRateParts` 2× in the two fixed components, while **parsed, both contain ZERO identifier nodes** for any of the six forbidden names. <details><summary>Previous status (plan 10-17 Tasks 1-2, superseded 2026-08-12)</summary>

Executing Phase 10 — plans 01-16 complete, **plan 17 paused at its human checkpoint**. **DS-13'S "FAILS THE BUILD" IS STRUCTURALLY MET FOR THE FIRST TIME, AND IT WAS WATCHED HAPPENING.** `package.json`'s `build` is now `npm run lint && npm run test:design && next build` — the load-bearing change, because Next 16 removed `next lint` and no longer runs ESLint during `next build` (L1) and `.github/workflows` does not exist (L2), so the npm script layer IS the gate boundary. `const LEAK_PROBE = "#ff0000";` injected at `src/components/ui/badge.tsx:18` — inside the vendored tree D-17 refuses to exempt — turned `npm run build` **red at exit 1**, `18:20  error  Raw design value "#ff0000" … fitout/no-raw-design-value`, `✖ 11 problems (1 error, 10 warnings)`, with `&&` correctly short-circuiting before the design gate and `next build`; `npm run test:design -- leak` then caught the same probe **independently of ESLint** at 1 failed / 17 passed; probe reverted, `npm run build` exit 0 in 72s. Both failing runs are pasted into `tests/design/leak.test.ts`'s own header, dated, naming what was injected and where. **Both gates read ONE list AND one SCOPE** — `eslint.config.mjs` imports `DESIGN_LEAK_PATTERNS` and spreads `LEAK_SCAN_GLOBS` into its `files:` key — with **zero new npm dependencies** (the plugin is inline; `git diff` on `package.json` shows exactly one changed line). **10-15's handoff is asserted three ways rather than honoured by omission**: `src/lib/design/**` is walked, is not scanned, and WOULD produce ≥40 violations if it were. D-17's positive control pins **30/30** vendored primitives visited. **THE HEADLINE FINDING IS THAT THE PLAN'S OWN DRIFT ALGORITHM WAS WRONG, and the tell was a finding neither of its two prescribed resolutions could close.** The naive cross product pairs `ui/calendar.tsx:221`'s `data-[range-middle=true]:text-foreground` with `data-[range-end=true]:bg-primary` — two mutually exclusive day states — at **~1.1:1**: adding the row would declare a 1.1:1 pairing legal, and "fix the component" means breaking a calendar that is not broken. So the CHECK was fixed, twice. **Narrowing A:** a foreground and a background pair only when their variant chains are identical or one is unconditional (`ui/dropdown-menu.tsx:76` does the same shape across a `not-` variant and the variant it negates). **Narrowing B:** `dark:` utilities are skipped because they cannot paint — `globals.css:32` scopes them to `&:is(.dark *)` and nothing activates `.dark` (provider writes `attribute="data-theme"` precisely to dodge that collision, `enableSystem` false, themes court/grove) — and because a skip is a hole unless its precondition is pinned, **all four mechanisms are asserted** and the header instructs that an activated `.dark` be answered with a THIRD THEME in the inventory, not a wider skip (watched red at 1 failed / 12 passed with `attribute="class"` substituted). What survived both narrowings is **one real undeclared pairing at two sites** — the inverted surface, `ui/tooltip.tsx:45` (`bg-foreground text-background`) and `listing/photo-uploader.tsx:278` (`bg-foreground/80`) — measured before being declared at **19.80/18.42** and **11.20/10.24** against a 4.5 bar, so **2 rows added** (29 → 31). Alias groups are **derived from token values, not typed**: the plan names two, the stylesheet has six. Drift watched red in BOTH directions — rows removed, and an undeclared pairing injected into a real component. **`npm run lint` measures 17s on this box, not the 85s the plan told me to record**; the accepted price of T-10-35 is ~29s of extra build, not 85. **D-9 DISPOSITIONED — accepted as debt** with the 119,835-byte figure reproduced by summing the emitted chunks (no rebuild): `@source not` would render `/dev/theme` unstyled, which is the very surface Task 3 is performed on, and a safelist is the second list D-16/D-18 exist to abolish; in context 10-12 took the stylesheet 134,132 → 119,079 and the route puts 1,103 back, so the phase still ships **−10.66%**. Design gate **375/375** (was 340), 20 files, DB-free; full DB suite **1197 passed / 4 skipped**, unchanged; `tsc` 0, build 0, lint **0 errors / 9 warnings** — byte-identical to the phase baseline with the new rule live. `npm run test:e2e` deliberately NOT re-run and the reason is stated: this plan changes no runtime code path (the only `src/` edit is two data rows in a module imported solely by `contrast.test.ts`), and D-6 item 1 has five byte-identical reproductions already. **DS-13 and DS-06 left Pending on purpose** — both are satisfied by Tasks 1-2, but Task 3 asks the developer to accept or reject D-11 (the accent deepening), and a rejection is a scope decision that could re-derive `--brand` and move every number DS-06 rests on. **NEXT: a human must open `/dev/theme` and rule on SC#3, D-11 and DS-04.** One correction for whoever reads Task 3's text: its `<what-built>` says "pinning the vendored 56" — the pinned number is **54**, and has been since 10-07 removed two dark-mode twins alongside two accessibility defects.

</details>

<details><summary>Previous status (plan 10-16, superseded 2026-08-12)</summary>

Executing Phase 10 — plans 01-16 complete. **THEME-04 IS CLOSED, and the phase's one real screen exists.** `/dev/theme` renders two themes SIDE BY SIDE IN NESTED `[data-theme]` subtrees — nine sections per pane, real booking components fed by identical fixtures, one `ThemePane` function called twice so a difference between the columns can only have come from the theme — and it returns **404 in a production build**, verified by `npm run build && npm start` (`/login` returned 200 in the same run). **A real Chromium confirms the whole contract travels through nesting, not just colour:** the grove pane paints brand `lab(48.18 -30.43 -6.04)` (teal) against court's `lab(49.17 65.89 41.55)` (coral), radius 28px against 14px, display 34px/700 against 28px/600, a radius ladder of 12–52px against 6–26px, and ink-tinted elevation at 2/16/-2px against court's pure black at 1/8/-1px. Zero duplicate DOM ids, nine sections in each pane, and no horizontal overflow at 320 / 375 / 1024px with court first. **THE HEADLINE FINDING IS NOT ABOUT THEMES AT ALL, AND IT WAS PREDICTED IN WRITING.** `type-scale.test.ts`'s own NOT COVERED header, written by 10-11, lists "it cannot see `cn()`/tailwind-merge precedence at a call site". This page is the first surface in the repo to use `text-heading`, `text-body` or `text-label`, and tailwind-merge classifies ALL FOUR named roles as text COLOURS — its font-size matcher rejects `display|heading|body|label`, its colour matcher accepts anything — so `cn("text-label", "text-muted-foreground")` returned **only the colour**. The role was being silently DELETED, with `tsc` green, lint green, the leak gate green and the source scan green (the class NAME is right; it just never survives to the DOM), and the element rendering at the INHERITED size. Same defect class as the slash-modifier trap, with a sharper edge. Fixed by registering the four under Tailwind's own `--text-*` namespace in `cn()`; +6 assertions (29 → 35) watched red at **5 failed / 30 passed** with the registration emptied, and the colour control stayed green — which is what makes it a size registration rather than a blanket `text-*` exemption. Nothing shipped moved: all 12 `sm:text-display` sites are plain strings that never reach a merge, full DB suite 1197 unchanged, 17 E2E specs unchanged. **The THEME-04 gate is 16 assertions across two files and the primary layer is the COMPILED stylesheet**, because that is the only artifact where a dropped `inline` is legible: three utilities must emit `var(--background)` / `var(--brand)` / `var(--radius)`, and the whole 130KB output must contain **zero** `var(--color-`. Watched red — `inline` deleted → **EXIT=1, 5 failed / 11 passed**, alias count **201** — and **the instructive half is what stayed GREEN**: every structural assertion (both theme selectors unlayered and source-ordered, both nested panes on the page) and **all four jsdom cases** passed against a build in which nested theming was completely dead. That is RESEARCH Pitfall 3 measured rather than described, and it is recorded in both test headers. The jsdom layer exists because 10-02's spike verdict reads `jsdom RESOLVES nested custom properties`, and it honours both of that verdict's conditions: raw `[data-theme]` blocks lifted VERBATIM by a CSS parse (never compiled output, which jsdom's `@layer` blindness would make vacuous) and `getPropertyValue` on the token (never a resolved colour, which jsdom never substitutes). **10-12's `shadow-sticky` zero moved here, in this plan's own commit, exactly as 10-12 said it would** — a one-entry inventory naming this route, and a compiled-output ABSENCE that becomes a presence resolving through `var(--elevation-sticky)`; the half of the zero that mattered (no PRODUCT surface uses the step) is still what the map says. Four smaller deviations, all Rule 1/3: a fourth file was required because a Server Component cannot hand `SlotPicker` its callback; the selected run is two real clicks ONE FRAME APART (same-tick clicks both read the empty state and re-anchor); `STICKY_INVENTORY` already existed in the same file for the z-index clause; and jsdom re-serialises the alpha slash in a multi-token custom property. Design gate **340/340** (was 318 at 10-15's close), DB-free, ~6.1s; full DB suite 1197 passed / 4 skipped; `tsc` 0, build 0, lint 0 errors / 9 warnings — all unchanged from baseline. `npm run test:e2e` 17 passed / 1 failed (D-6 item 1, a **fifth** byte-identical signal). **D-9 opened and measured rather than waved away:** the preview's utilities ship to production where the route 404s — 118,732 → 119,835 bytes on clean rebuilds of both sides, **+1,103 (+0.93%)** — with both removal mechanisms priced and rejected. Next: 10-17.

</details>

<details><summary>Previous status (plan 10-15, superseded 2026-08-12)</summary>

Executing Phase 10 — plans 01-15 complete. **DS-12 AND DS-14 ARE BOTH CLOSED, and neither was left Pending — the first two requirements this phase has been able to finish outright** (DS-02, DS-03 and DS-13 each had a clause claimed by a later plan; these do not). **The drift DS-12 exists to close was real and shipped:** `listing-map.tsx:22` carried `const BRAND_CORAL = "#E8484E"` annotated "matches the UI-SPEC accent", and it had not matched `--brand` since 10-03 re-derived that token for contrast — the stylesheet painted `#da2d34`, the map painted something else, and nothing in the repository could tell. `grep -Ec '#[0-9a-fA-F]{3,8}\b'` over that whole file now returns **0**, comments included. `scripts/generate-design-tokens.mjs` reads `globals.css` through the ONE shared parser and writes three committed artifacts — a 23-token-per-theme module with oklch + 8-bit hex, and two themed favicons — deterministically (`wrote 0/3` on a second run, porcelain empty). **It reproduced 10-03's derivation independently to the byte** (court `#da2d34`, grove `#13807c`), which is the cross-check a file compared against itself can never give; `--ring` landing on `#555555` matches the value `focus-recipe.test.ts`'s header had computed by hand. **D-18's "CI check" now exists in the only place it can** — there is no `.github/workflows`, so `tests/design/token-drift.test.ts` IS the regen-diff gate (7 assertions, byte equality with NO normalisation, watched red at **1 failed / 6 passed** on a one-character hex edit and green again after regenerating). **THE HEADLINE FINDING IS ONE NO LAYER OF THIS PHASE'S TESTING COULD SEE.** The plan's favicon shape — a `metadata.icons` entry plus a `useEffect` that re-points the link — passes `tsc`, lint, all 318 design assertions and `next build`, and puts a correct icon in the server HTML. Probed against a **production** build it ships **TWO** `rel="icon"` links whenever the theme is not court, **with the stale one LAST**: `["/icon-grove.svg", "/icon-court.svg"]`. Browsers commonly honour the last, so the tab shows court while the app renders grove — D-19 failing silently, through exactly the ambiguity the plan gives as its reason for deleting `favicon.ico`. The alternative shape (the component rendering a `<link>` for React to hoist) leaks the same way in the other direction, because hoisted metadata links are keyed by href so a change ADDS one and never removes the first. **The generalisable rule: a `<link>` React owns cannot be re-pointed at a different file.** All three shapes were built and measured; the icon now has ONE owner (no metadata entry, a plain element nothing else manages) — exactly one link in every state, still one after two navigations and a hard reload, `/favicon.ico` 404. The cost is stated rather than waved away: **252ms** of the browser's generic glyph on a cold load. **A consequence for 10-17:** `grep -c "icons" src/app/layout.tsx >= 1` now passes on the COMMENT that argues against the entry, not on the entry — recorded rather than quietly enjoyed. **Six files deleted**, each re-verified unreferenced immediately before removal; the lone `next.svg` hit is `ui/calendar.tsx:35`'s `rdp-button_next>svg`, a confirmed substring false positive. `.gitattributes` pins the three generated artifacts to `eol=lf` — `text=auto` plus `core.autocrlf` would hand a fresh Windows clone CRLF and turn the byte-comparison red for a reason that is not drift (git's own "LF will be replaced by CRLF" warning fired for the unpinned script and stayed silent for the three pinned files). `scaffold-residue.test.ts` extended 8 → **19** and watched red three ways. Design gate **318/318** (was 300), DB-free, ~5.0s; full DB suite 1197 passed / 4 skipped, unchanged; `tsc` 0, build 0, lint 0 errors / 9 warnings — all unchanged from baseline. `npm run test:e2e` 17 passed / 1 failed twice (D-6 item 1, a **fourth** byte-identical signal), and `public-listing.spec.ts` — the spec rendering the Leaflet map whose pin this plan rewires — passes 3/3 standalone. Next: 10-16.

</details>

<details><summary>Previous status (plan 10-14, superseded 2026-08-12)</summary>

Executing Phase 10 — plans 01-14 complete. **THEME-05 IS CLOSED, and DS-13's violations are at zero ahead of the gate that will enforce them.** Zero `dark:` occurrences survive under `src/app/**` or `src/components/**` outside the vendored tree — all 10 removed from the 5 files the plan named, in exactly the distribution it predicted — and the last 18 raw palette-class CODE occurrences across 6 files are rewritten onto semantic tokens, including the ONE vendored site (`ui/dialog.tsx`'s overlay) that D-17 refuses to exempt. **The headline finding is that the number this plan was told to pin had been wrong for seven plans, and the correction was already written down.** The plan says **56** in four places (`must_haves`, the Task 2 action's `toBe(56)`, its acceptance criteria, its `artifacts.contains`); the tree holds **54**. <details><summary>Previous status (plan 10-13, superseded 2026-08-12)</summary>

Executing Phase 10 — plans 01-13 complete. **DS-03 IS CLOSED — both clauses.** 10-12 landed the shadow half; this plan landed the z-index half, and every stacking decision in the app now reads from a four-step named scale instead of from two magic numbers. **20 code call sites migrated across 12 files** — 11 sticky (9 positive + 2 negative) and 9 dialog — with the 2 `z-0` on `calendar.tsx`'s range endpoints deliberately untouched, and **zero raw `z-10`, `-z-10` or `z-50` left anywhere in `src/`**, verified in the source AND in the emitted stylesheet. **The single most important finding is that following the plan verbatim would have shipped a visible regression its own gate could not catch.** The plan, `10-UI-SPEC.md` and every count downstream of them read `z-10 ×12`, because `grep -E '\bz-10\b'` **matches inside `-z-10`** — `-` is a word boundary. Two of those twelve are the "or" dividers on `/login` and `/signup`: a rule that must render BEHIND the label it crosses. Task 1's literal instruction ("replace all 5 `z-10` occurrences with `z-(--z-sticky)`") would have moved both from −10 to +10, in front of their own text, on the two highest-traffic unauthenticated pages in the app. They map to `-z-(--z-sticky)` instead, compiling to `calc(var(--z-sticky) * -1)` — value-preserving, still reading from the scale — and the gate counts the negative form as its OWN inventory so a future flattening fails a named assertion rather than passing a total. **The mapped total is 20, not the plan's 21, and the 21st is the twelfth and most dangerous form of this phase's grep-versus-comment collision**: `globals.css`'s CONSUMPTION FORM comment quotes `z-(--z-dialog)` verbatim while explaining why the bare form fails, so the raw grep lands on exactly the predicted number by a *different composition*. Every prior occurrence produced a WRONG number, which gets noticed; this one produced the RIGHT number for the wrong reason. (A 12th phantom site — `host-booking-row.tsx:121`'s comment quoting `relative z-10` — was rewritten descriptively, the convention `booking-row.tsx:111` had already established for exactly this.) **The consumption form was compiled and verified before a single edit**: `z-(--z-sticky)` → `z-index: var(--z-sticky)`, `-z-(--z-sticky)` → `calc(var(--z-sticky) * -1)`, and `z-dialog` / `z-sticky` → **nothing at all**, even force-safelisted. Tailwind v4 has no z-index namespace, so the readable-looking bare form is not an error, not a warning, and not a rule — it is silence, and that is now an assertion rather than a comment. **The `z-50` mapping is NOT value-preserving and that is deliberate**: `--z-dialog` is 30, so all nine former `z-50` layers moved DOWN together; `dialog.tsx`'s overlay and content were both 50 and are both 30, with DOM order deciding exactly as before, so the dialog still renders above its own overlay. Gate: `tests/design/elevation-z.test.ts` 31 → **53** assertions, **watched go red TWICE because the two failure modes have measurably different blast radii** — a `z-50` reinstated at `popover.tsx` gives **6 failed / 47 passed**, while DELETING the z-index from that same line gives **2 failed / 51 passed**, since every zero-violations assertion passes perfectly against a tree with no stacking at all. That second number is the whole argument for pinning exact counts on both sides, measured rather than asserted for the first time this phase. Two auto-fixes to `compile-css.ts`: its safelist sanitiser rejected every parenthesis and therefore the ONLY legal spelling of a named z layer, so it was widened — and because it is a security check, the break-out property it exists for is now **asserted** (six hostile shapes rejected) instead of documented only. Design gate 292/292 (was 270), DB-free, ~4.7s; full DB suite 1197 passed / 4 skipped, unchanged; build, `tsc` and lint all unchanged from baseline. `npm run test:e2e` 17 passed / 1 failed — **D-6 item 1, re-proven pre-existing** by checking all twelve source files out at `45ecf07` and reproducing an identical signal, because a stacking remap is the one change class that could genuinely put an overlay above its content. Next: 10-14.

</details>

<details><summary>Previous status (plan 10-12, superseded 2026-08-12)</summary>

Executing Phase 10 — plans 01-12 complete. **Every shadow in the app now maps to a named elevation step, and the stylesheet that ships contains only what the app uses.** The 14 call sites matched the plan exactly — 4 raised (`shadow-xs` ×1 + `shadow-sm` ×3), 5 overlay (`shadow-md` ×4 + `shadow-lg` ×1), 5 `shadow-none` deliberately untouched, across 10 files. This matters because Tailwind's default `shadow-md` compiles to a **literal**, not a `var()`: a theme block redeclaring `--shadow-md` does nothing, so all 14 surfaces were frozen against every theme and grove's pronounced elevation (D-02) could not reach them. `shadow-sticky` is left with **zero** call sites on purpose — reserved for bottom-anchored bars, none exist, and the zero is asserted so nobody invents a home for it; 10-16's `/dev/theme` ladder is its exerciser. **The plan's raw-grep criteria were ALL wrong against the tree, from one cause, and it is a new form of the phase's recurring collision**: the greps include `globals.css`, whose three `--shadow-*` TOKEN DECLARATIONS and explanatory comment are counted as call sites (raised 5 not 4, overlay 6 not 5, none 7 not 5, sticky 1 not 0). Eleven occurrences now, and the first where the artifact tripping the criterion was **the contract being tested**, not prose about it — fixed with a `(?<![\w-])` lookbehind. The `dark:` pin is **54**, not the plan's 56; D-2 already recorded the drop after 10-07, and it was proven unmoved rather than adjusted to fit. Gate: `tests/design/elevation-z.test.ts` 13 → **31** assertions, **watched go red at 5 failed / 26 passed** on a reinstated `shadow-md`, with the COUNT assertion firing — which is what makes a delete-instead-of-rename fail as loudly as a wrong name. **DEFERRED ITEM D-1 IS CLOSED**, the phase's oldest, nominated to this plan by 10-04 and 10-09 and owned by no later plan. One directive — `@import "tailwindcss" source("../")` — narrows Tailwind's content root from the REPOSITORY to `src/`. Measured on clean rebuilds: **134,132 → 119,079 bytes (−15,053, −11.2%), 119 dead selectors removed, 0 added, and 0 of the 119 used anywhere in `src/**/*.tsx`** (verified twice: a strict standalone-token scan, plus hand-checking the 8 highest-risk entries and confirming their real prefixed forms still emit). **The bytes are the least of it — removing the prose made two tests go red that had been green all phase, neither a regression.** (1) `@tailwindcss/postcss` caches its design system keyed on the INPUT FILE PATH, so every `compileGlobalsCssWith()` after the first silently returned the first one's output and `@source inline` was ignored; 10-04's "the default shadows are still literal" **control** had been passing because a markdown file said `shadow-md`. (2) Three DS-01 assertions read `.font-sans`/`.font-mono` rules no `src/` file uses — `font-sans` reaches the app via `@apply`, which inlines the declaration; DS-01 was never broken and is now asserted on the `html` rule and on `.font-heading`, which has two real call sites. **Compiled-output assertions are sound in this repo for the first time**, and DS-03 immediately takes the strongest form available: no default shadow rule in the emitted stylesheet, from any source — guarded by a control that the scan still reaches `src/`, since a root narrowed one level too far makes every absence assertion pass against an app shipping no CSS at all. Design gate 270/270 (was 251), DB-free, ~4.4s; full DB suite 1197 passed / 4 skipped, unchanged. DS-03 left **Pending** — its z-index clause is 10-13's declared part 2. Next: 10-13.

<details><summary>Previous status (plan 10-10, superseded 2026-08-12)</summary>

Executing Phase 10 — plans 01-10 complete. **DS-10 IS CLOSED, and the status vocabulary is now a TYPE rather than a claim.** Two tone vocabularies shipped and neither was the design system's: `booking-status.ts` declared a 3-value union, `payout-ledger-status.ts` a 4-value one, and the value they shared was a filled green chip measuring 3.24:1. Both are now typed against ONE `StatusTone` — `neutral / positive / attention / soft-accent` — with `STATUS_TONE_RECIPES` as a TOTAL `Record` over it. **The compile gate was watched, not assumed:** a fifth tone without a recipe produced `TS2741: Property 'warning' is missing … but required in type 'Record<…>'`, `tsc` exit 2, then reverted, and the verbatim error is recorded in the test header. D-14 is enforced STRUCTURALLY: the three lifecycle tones share `bg-muted` + `text-foreground` and differ ONLY in a distinct icon hue, so a `positive` whose text carried the hue fails a test before any pixel is drawn. **FOUR filled-green chips were retired, not the three the plan's own line references implied** — confirmed, paid, payouts-enabled and the listing card's Live badge; the survivor count is pinned to the ONE glyph-only wizard step marker BY NAME (3.83/3.84 non-text, the single legal `--success-foreground` pairing). TWO of the four had NO ICON AT ALL and were therefore colour-only: the payouts-enabled banner (the plan flagged this) and the Live listing chip (the plan said "keep the badge's existing icon" — there was none). Both gained one. The gate (`tests/design/status-vocab.test.ts`, 13 assertions) was watched go RED on a deliberately reinstated `paid` fill — 3 failed / 10 passed, each failure naming the file, and the type layer correctly unmoved. Class matching is BOUNDARY-SAFE (`text-success(?![\w-])`) because a plain substring check reads `text-success-foreground` as `text-success` and would have reported the wizard as a positive call site. Design gate 239/239 (was 226), DB-free, ~5s; full DB suite 1197/1197 unchanged. Two shipped suites asserted the retired tone names and were rewritten to assert the MEANINGS instead (approved is not the paid signal; only confirmed is). Replacement classes verified to COMPILE from the production stylesheet, not merely to be present in source. Next: 10-11. NOTE — `soft-accent` is the one tone declared with no adopter WIRED: `spots-left-chip.tsx` already renders those exact three classes literally, but rewiring it moves a count `brand-recipe.test.ts` pins by name, so it is logged as D-7 rather than absorbed. The grep-versus-comment collision was AVOIDED for the first time by design in three separate places (the tone module's header, the wizard's exemption comment, and the two retyped view modules) — each names things descriptively instead of quoting the string its own criterion counts.

## Accumulated Context

### Roadmap Evolution

- **v1.1 roadmap created (2026-08-11) — Phases 10–19, numbering continued from v1.0's Phase 9.** Derived from the research's reconciled build order (SUMMARY § Implications for Roadmap / ARCHITECTURE § Build Order), which two researchers reached independently. Two deliberate departures from that shape, both stated with reasons: (1) the research's **group/open-capacity** phase was folded into **Phase 13** because REQUIREMENTS.md defines no separate REQ-IDs for it — a standalone phase would carry zero requirements; the open-capacity pass picker sits in Phase 12 as a pre-hold listing surface and `/invite/[token]` in Phase 13 as a post-booking artifact. (2) The research's standalone **email** phase was merged into **Phase 15** because PITFALLS' own phase taxonomy already places the email shell inside `P-AUTH` (its risk table maps rows 14 and 15 to P-AUTH), and three requirements do not justify a phase at `standard` granularity — EMAIL-02's only dependency (DS-12) still lands in Phase 10, so nothing is delayed. Also split the research's single **booker core** phase into 12 (search → listing → checkout) and 13 (confirmation → bookings → trust) because the combined phase carried 21 requirements. **MAP (18) and HOURS (19) are their own phases per D-136** and are never to be folded into a surface-polish phase.

- Phase 9 added (2026-07-27): **Open-Capacity Bookings** — the second host-set occupancy mode (drop-in / common-use, capacity-counter availability), split out of the Phase-8 group-booking discussion as its own phase because it stresses AVAILABILITY (a concurrent seat-claim), not payment. Each visitor books single-payer on the existing rail; the new work is a capacity-counter that replaces the GiST exclusion constraint for these listings, race-proven. Adds OPEN-01..04; depends on Phase 8's `occupancy_mode` seam (default `exclusive`). Organizer-driven open play / cost-split (GPAY-01) remains deferred.

### Decisions

Decisions are logged in PROJECT.md Key Decisions table.
Recent decisions affecting current work:

- [12-08]: **`[11-13]` was a DEV-MODE ARTEFACT, measured on a 2×2 matrix rather than a before/after.** Same seeded not-payable listing, same theme seed, `npm run build && npm start` vs `npm run dev`, with the `TooltipProvider` site both RESTORED and DELETED: present → dev `Hydration failed × 1` / prod **0**; deleted → **0 / 0**. Running the discriminator on the mutated tree as well as the fixed one is what turned a claimable repair into the correct finding — the production build never had the mismatch, so `[11-03]`/`[11-11](a)`/`[11-14]` resolve together as one dev-mode class. The deletion still stands on its own merits and it did fix `e2e/availability.spec.ts:261` (a standing red since 12-06), because in dev the regenerated tree was dropping the `Not bookable yet` CTA.
- [12-08]: **The two standing reds on `/listings/[id]` are NOT one investigation.** 12-02 and 12-06 both recorded them as *"plausibly one investigation"* of the route's streaming behaviour. Measured: the draft-404 case fails identically under a production build (`curl` → 200 in both modes; an unmatched URL correctly 404s in both), so it survives the discriminator that the hydration mismatch does not. It is a real rendering-strategy defect needing its own Rule 4 decision, and nothing that fixes a hydration boundary can close it.
- [12-08]: **An acceptance criterion phrased as a directory grep is wider than the property it names — measure it and report both dispositions rather than deleting another plan's surface.** *"No `components/ui/tooltip` import under `src/components/{search,listing,availability,booking}`"* still matches two files after the listing page's site was deleted: `photo-uploader.tsx` (a HOST surface, not the booker path) and `slot-picker.tsx` (genuinely booker-path, whose tooltip carries the notice requirement and has nowhere else to live on a wrapping grid of 44px chips). Deferred to **12-09**, which already opens that file, with the copy/placement question stated. 12-UI-SPEC's *"D-56 deletes the last one"* is true only of the listing page.
- [12-08]: **A whole-text negative assertion must be probed against the shape `textContent` really produces.** The obvious `\b\d+ (courts|rooms|spaces)` was green for the very defect it names, because `textContent` concatenates siblings with no separator (`…Units4 courts`) and there is no word boundary between a letter and a digit. Under the mutation, five positive cases fired and the one written for the bare claim did not. Fixed with `(?<!\d)` and a guard-the-guard line pinning the concatenated string.
- [11-21]: **A streamed page's PENDING state is a prefix of its own response, not a timing window.** The plan prescribed "stall the session request with `page.route` and a delay"; there is no such request — `auth.api.getSession({ headers: await headers() })` runs on the SERVER inside the `<Suspense>` boundary, so the browser makes exactly one request and a delay moves both states equally. `e2e/helpers/served-document.ts` intercepts the document, fetches it, and fulfils with everything before React's first `<div hidden id="S:…">` completion segment: real server bytes, cookies replayed, zero timing. Measured on `/` — 106,723 bytes, marker at 26,866, prefix holds `AuthSlotSkeleton` and not "Log in". The cut offset is ASSERTED, because a marker that stopped matching would make pending and resolved the same document and every equality over the pair vacuously true.
- [11-21]: **The prescribed no-shift probe was vacuous, and the fix was to assert the box the constant actually pins.** Deleting `AUTH_SLOT_BOX`'s `min-w-44` left all twelve prescribed header/brand equalities green — `ml-auto … justify-end` makes the slot the last flex child, so resolution moves only its own left edge and no mutation to the reservation can reach the brand. `site-chrome.tsx` already said so in words. `site-auth-slot`'s own box was added as a third assertion; the same mutation then reddened 2 of 6, measuring the resolved cluster at 146.88 (court) / 155.05 (grove) against a 140 fallback — and only on the PUBLIC composition, because the booker and host slots hold the bell's own box in both states.
- [11-21]: **A rendered geometry gate asserts what the constant CLAIMS, never what would make the numbers match.** The card-grid comparison is at the MEDIA box (`RESULT_CARD_MEDIA` vs `<AspectRatio ratio={4 / 3}>` — 179.33 × 134.48, identical in both themes) and not at the cell, because `card-grid-skeleton.tsx` states its text bars are proportions of the cell rather than measurements of anything real; the cells legitimately differ by ~162px, and by 12px BETWEEN themes. The panel is width-plus-a-FLOOR because `PANEL_MIN_HEIGHT` is documented as a floor and the resolved panel is 22px taller. The plan's literal "width and height within ±2px" would have been satisfiable only by padding a placeholder until a gate went green.
- [11-21]: **A source gate that reads props off `JsxAttribute` nodes is blind to a spread, and a preview must not hide behind that.** `/dev/theme`'s `tone="positive"` empty state, authored as `<EmptyState {...FIXTURE} />`, was counted as a call site but was invisible to `empty-state-adoption.test.ts`'s tree-wide positive-tone assertion — green by not being seen. Both call sites were rewritten with inline attributes until the gate reddened, and the assertion was widened from a bare count of 1 into a named two-entry set with reasons PLUS a separate `src/app/dev/**`-excluding clause that keeps the PRODUCT claim at exactly one surface. The general hole is logged in `deferred-items.md` rather than relied on.
- [11-21]: **`e2e/skeleton-geometry.spec.ts` lives in the default `chromium` project, not `visual`** (a deviation from `11-VALIDATION.md`, recorded in the file). A `boundingBox` comparison needs no baseline — nothing to store, commit or regenerate — and the `visual` project is not created at all off Linux (D-29), so putting it there would make it unrunnable on the machine where skeletons are written.
- [11-20]: **A response that must never be non-2xx has to be rendered to BYTES before the status line is committed.** `next/og`'s `ImageResponse` extends `Response` and runs satori inside the body stream's `start()`, so the constructor returns with headers already on the wire and a `try`/`catch` around `return new ImageResponse(...)` — the shape every tutorial shows — catches nothing. `src/app/og-render.ts` awaits `.arrayBuffer()` and returns a fresh `Response`. Three rungs, each sharing less I/O than the one above it (own fonts → `next/og`'s bundled font → a 1×1 PNG constant), all three watched firing. The remaining hole is named: a module that fails to PARSE is still a 500, and only `npm run build` catches that.
- [11-20]: **The invite share image takes no argument at all, rather than an ignored one.** An unread parameter can be read again tomorrow; a signature that declares nothing cannot be handed the token without an edit somebody has to make on purpose. Paired with an import ALLOW-list (two modules) rather than the prescribed denylist over `@/lib/db` / `@/lib/group`, which a relative path walks around without anyone intending to. Both asserted on the AST by `tests/design/og-routes.test.ts`; the file's header describes all three identifiers **by role and never by name**, because a comment quoting them is what would make the one-command `grep` audit permanently non-zero.
- [11-20]: **The invite page's metadata MAY vary by token; the invite image may not — and the line between them is whether the surface adds a bit that was not already readable.** The page body has always differed between a live invite and an inactive one (showing the event is the product), so a venue in the title reveals nothing new; 08-06's actual property — unknown = revoked = voided = cancelled = malformed as one indistinguishable state — is preserved, with all three inactive causes emitting byte-identical title and description. The image is a NEW unauthenticated endpoint fetched automatically by every intermediary, so it is constant and was measured byte-identical across five token classes.
- [11-20]: **Two Next-16 metadata landmines where the thorough-looking edit is the broken one.** Declaring `openGraph` at all deletes the file-convention `og:image` and downgrades `twitter:card` to `summary` (measured both ways, one route, nothing else changed); and the officially documented OG font load, `fetch(new URL(...))`, fails on the Node runtime with `fetch failed` because undici implements no `file:` scheme — shipping every card in the wrong face at a 200. Both are recorded in `deferred-items.md` because both will be reintroduced by the next person who touches metadata.
- [11-19]: **Identical markup is not an identical response, so copy parity alone would have shipped an open oracle.** Next serves a `not-found.tsx` boundary with 404 and the invite page's inactive branch with 200, and no `not-found.tsx` can set a status. A human cannot tell them apart; a script reading status codes tells them apart instantly — and a script is the only thing that walks a 20-symbol token space. The control that closes it is UNREACHABILITY: nothing under `src/app/(public)/invite/**` calls `notFound()`, asserted by `tests/design/invite-notfound-parity.test.ts` rather than left to a comment. Measured against `next start`: a malformed token and two well-formed unknown ones return byte-identical 200s once the token is substituted out.
- [11-19]: **Two files that must keep looking alike is a security property maintained by POLICY.** The invite 404 and the invite inactive branch render ONE component (`InviteInactive`, extracted with `InviteCard` to `src/components/group/invite-card.tsx`); the copy stays a PROP (11-09's `EmptyState` rule) so both call sites still import the constants and the gate has something per-file to assert. The `card-pattern-coverage.test.ts` inventory row moved with the component, in the same commit, exactly as that gate's failure message asks.
- [11-19]: **A gate asserted over imports is deliberately green when the shared value changes — so the words need exactly one pin, and it must not be deleted by the hoist that created the gate.** `tests/group/rsvp-rate-limit.test.ts` keeps its literal and gains one assertion that the literal still names what `submitRsvp` composes; a reword turns that file 6 failed while the design gate correctly stays green.
- [11-19]: **The root not-found is part of every route's tree, so its import graph decides whether the WHOLE app prerenders.** Composing `PublicHeader` there (measured) flipped `/_not-found` AND `/dev/theme` to `ƒ` — zero static routes left in the build. It composes `SiteChrome` + a new DB-free `AnonymousAuthActions` instead, and the accepted cost is that a signed-in visitor sees `Log in` / `Sign up` on that one page.

- [10-15]: **A `<link>` React owns cannot be re-pointed at a different file — in EITHER direction — and the failure is invisible to every static layer this phase has built.** Measured against a production build, not the dev server. Shape A (a `metadata.icons` entry plus an effect that mutates the element Next rendered): React loses track of its own node and re-creates it, leaving TWO `rel="icon"` links with the STALE one LAST. Shape B (the component returning a `<link>` for React to hoist): hoisted metadata links are keyed by href, so a change ADDS a second and never removes the first. Both ship a page whose tab identity is chosen by the user agent — the exact ambiguity deleting `favicon.ico` was meant to remove — and shape A resolves the WRONG way, showing court while the app renders grove. `tsc`, lint, 318 design assertions and `next build` all passed on it. **Rule: the tab icon has exactly ONE owner, and any assertion about what a file CONTAINS cannot see what a framework DOES with it at runtime.** The cost of single ownership is measured and accepted: 252ms of the browser's generic glyph on a cold load.
- [10-15]: **A generated artifact that is byte-compared must be pinned to `eol=lf` in `.gitattributes`.** This repo declares `* text=auto` and runs with `core.autocrlf=true`, so a fresh Windows clone rewrites the working tree to CRLF and D-18's no-normalisation drift check goes red for a reason that is not drift. Confirmed by git's own behaviour: `git add` warned "LF will be replaced by CRLF" for the unpinned generator script and said nothing for the three pinned artifacts. A drift check that fails for the wrong reason is a drift check that gets weakened.
- [10-15]: **The grep-versus-comment collision reached fifteen occurrences, and the fifteenth inverts it: a criterion now passes on a comment that argues AGAINST the code it was counting.** `grep -c "icons" src/app/layout.tsx >= 1` is satisfied by the paragraph explaining why the metadata entry is deliberately absent. Recorded rather than quietly enjoyed — the criterion's intent (no icon-less first paint) is traded, deliberately and with a measurement, for an unambiguous tab identity. The fourteenth was the familiar direction, inside a test written in the same task: an assertion that an icon carries no typeface attribute tripped on the icon's own comment explaining why it must not; resolved by stripping the XML comment and asserting against the MARKUP.
- [10-15]: DS-12 and DS-14 both marked **Complete** — the first two requirements this phase has closed outright rather than holding `Pending` for a later plan's clause (DS-02, DS-03 and DS-13 each split across two plans). DS-12's text names `email` and `global-error` as future consumers: `src/lib/email.ts` has zero hex literals and `src/app/global-error.tsx` does not exist (STATE-02, Phase 11), so the module is SHAPED for them and wires the one consumer that exists, which is all of the requirement reachable today. **For 10-17: `src/lib/design/**` must be excluded from DS-13's raw-value leak scan** — `tokens.generated.ts` carries 46 hex literals by design, legal precisely because they are generated and byte-checked.
- [10-15]: **The generator reproduced 10-03's colour derivation independently, to the byte** (court `--brand` → `#da2d34`, grove → `#13807c`), matching what `contrast.test.ts` pins with hand-rolled WCAG maths. That agreement between two code paths written five plans apart is the cross-check a file compared against itself can never provide, and it is why the drift test's blind spot ("if the generator's colour maths were wrong, both sides would be wrong together") is survivable.
- [10-13]: **A SIGN IS PART OF A VALUE, and a `\b` boundary assertion does not know that.** `grep -E '\bz-10\b'` matches inside `-z-10`, so the `z-10 ×12` inventory in `10-UI-SPEC.md` — inherited verbatim by 10-13's plan and by every acceptance criterion in it — silently folded two NEGATIVE dividers in with ten positive lifts. Following Task 1 literally would have moved `/login` and `/signup`'s "or" rules from `z-index: -10` to `+10`, in front of their own text, and the gate would still have counted 21. This is a NEW failure class for the phase: unlike the eleven grep-versus-comment collisions, nothing was written incorrectly and no prose was involved — it is a property of the regex alone. **Rule for every future inventory: count a negative utility as its own name, never merged into its positive twin.**
- [10-13]: **The grep-versus-comment collision reached twelve occurrences and its most dangerous form yet — it produced the RIGHT number for the wrong reason.** `grep -rho -- "z-(--z-" src/app src/components | wc -l` returns exactly the 21 the plan predicted, as 20 real code sites plus `globals.css`'s CONSUMPTION FORM comment quoting `z-(--z-dialog)` while explaining why the bare form fails. Every prior occurrence produced a wrong number, which gets noticed. A gate written against that raw total would have been permanently green while believing something false about its own composition.
- [10-13]: DS-03 marked **Complete** — the second requirement this phase closed across two plans by design (10-12's shadow clause, 10-13's z clause), the split 10-04's deviation 2 predicted and 10-12 deliberately held the ID `Pending` for.
- [10-13]: **Observe red TWICE when a gate claims to catch two different failure modes.** A `z-50` reinstated at `popover.tsx` fails 6 assertions; DELETING the z-index from that same line fails only 2 — the count and the per-file inventory — because every zero-violations assertion passes perfectly against a tree with no stacking at all. The 4-assertion gap is the exact size of the hole the count assertions fill, and it is the first time this phase has measured that rather than argued it.
- [10-13]: **A validator that cannot express the only legal form of the thing under test must be widened, not routed around.** `compile-css.ts`'s safelist sanitiser rejected every parenthesis, and `z-(--z-dialog)` is the ONLY spelling of a named z layer (Tailwind v4 has no z-index namespace). Widened — and because it is a security check guarding a CSS string interpolation, the break-out property it exists for is now ASSERTED (six hostile shapes rejected) rather than documented only. A widened validator with no test on it is how a validator keeps widening.
- [10-11]: **The grep-versus-comment collision is now a TEN-time pattern, and 10-11 is the first plan where it broke a criterion in BOTH directions at once.** A presence control was too low (`text-[0.8rem]` counts 5 lines in `src/components/ui`, not the plan's 4 — `button.tsx:42` is 10-08's comment explaining the exemption) and an absence criterion was falsely tripped (the forbidden slash-modifier grep over `src/app` hits `globals.css:67`, which is 10-04's warning never to use the form). The false-positive direction is the familiar one; the false-negative direction is worse and is the new lesson: a raw-text POSITIVE CONTROL can be satisfied by prose alone, so deleting the real vendored site and leaving the comment would still have "proved" the scanner works. **Resolution for every future source-scan gate in this phase: strip comments before matching, and add a control on the control** — assert the raw text carries MORE occurrences than the code does, which fails loudly if the stripper ever becomes a no-op.
- [10-11]: **A comment stripper inside a violation scanner must be line-oriented, never a `/*…*/` regex.** Measured, not hypothetical: `src/app/(app)/profile/profile-form.tsx:109` carries `accept="image/*"` inside a JSX string and the next block terminator in that file is at line 195, so a naive block strip silently deletes 86 lines of real markup — and a scanner that eats a third of a file then reports it clean. A comment must OPEN its line (optionally in JSX braces) to be recognised, and a trailing `//` never truncates a line of code, because a violation could sit after a URL on the same line.
- [10-11]: DS-02 marked **Complete** — the first requirement in this phase whose two clauses were delivered by two different plans (10-04 the token contract, 10-11 the call-site migration), which is exactly the split 10-04's deviation 2 predicted when it deliberately left the ID `Pending`.
- [10-11]: The `sm:text-display` inventory is pinned PER FILE (8 files, 12 sites) rather than as a bare total, so a failure names which surface moved. Its blast radius was measured, not assumed: reinstating one literal turned exactly two assertions red and left all 17 token assertions green — which is the argument for the source scan in one line, since a theme contract cannot tell you whether anything obeys it.
- [10-11]: The 156 co-located `font-semibold` / `tracking-tight` / `leading-tight` call sites needed **zero edits**, as 10-04 designed. The migration is 14 one-word class swaps, not a 170-site refactor, because the weight/tracking/leading families are themselves per-theme tokens.
- [10-05]: The grep-versus-comment collision is now a FOUR-TIME pattern in this phase (10-01, 10-03, 10-04, and six separate times in 10-05). Stated as a phase lesson: any acceptance criterion of the form "identifier X must not appear in file Y" is in direct tension with the same plan's requirement to explain WHY X is absent — a comment describing a construct is textually indistinguishable from the construct. 10-05's resolution is the one to reuse in 10-06/10-07: honour the grep, keep every word of the reasoning by naming the identifier descriptively, and add one line recording that the literal is omitted BECAUSE its absence is grep-asserted (without that line the next reader "fixes" the circumlocution and silently breaks the criterion). The `toHaveScreenshot` case had real teeth — the UI-SPEC calls any Phase-10 occurrence a scope alarm, so an explanatory comment inside `e2e/helpers/theme.ts` would have tripped a phase-wide grep from inside the very helper that exists to tell Phase 11 how to shoot the baseline.
- [10-05]: `THEME_STORAGE_KEY` is IMPORTED by `e2e/helpers/theme.ts` from the provider, never duplicated as the literal `"theme"`. A Playwright helper that seeds a key the provider does not read is a silent no-op with NO symptom: Phase 11's swap smoke would go green while screenshotting the same theme twice, and Phase 17's axe pass would audit court twice and report full two-theme coverage. The seam is therefore created in the phase that owns the key, not the phase that first needs it.
- [10-05]: THEME-01 marked `Complete` (both clauses — provider mounted on `data-theme` never `class`, and the toast mapping named themes instead of passing them through — are delivered and asserted). DS-14 deliberately left `Pending`: its "starter SVGs and default favicon removed" clause is 10-15's, and 10-15 must EXTEND `tests/design/scaffold-residue.test.ts` rather than create a second file (its header says so).
- [10-05]: The theme runtime was probed in a REAL browser rather than trusted from the test suite, because every assertion in that suite mocks `next-themes` and so proves the contract the app STATES, not that the library honours it. The probe closed T-10-02 with evidence (`?theme=evil"><script>` left the attribute at `court`) and confirmed zero hydration warnings across four navigations. The build output was checked for the corollary: the four static routes stayed `○ (Static)`, so reading `window.location.search` in an effect forced nothing dynamic.
- [10-06]: The Button CVA is THE contract for the rest of the phase — `variant="brand"`, `size="touch"` and the one focus recipe live in `src/components/ui/button.tsx` and are copied FROM there by 10-07 (10 more vendored files) and 10-08/10-09 (20 call sites). A colour string at a call site is now a defect, not a style.
- [10-06]: A hover that must darken is written `hover:bg-[color-mix(in_oklch,var(--brand),var(--foreground)_10%)]`, NEVER an alpha modifier. An alpha tint over a light surface lightens: `bg-brand/90` measures 4.04:1 (court) / 3.87:1 (grove) against a 4.5 bar, the mix form 5.41 / 5.36. `10-RESEARCH.md` § Code Examples still ships the alpha form and is superseded — `tests/design/button-variants.test.ts` asserts the negative so copying it verbatim fails a committed gate.
- [10-06]: The focus ring's `/50` had to go because no `--ring` value could ever fix it — at half alpha the lightest neutral reaching 3:1 still fails on `--muted` (2.93:1). The offset colour is declared explicitly because Tailwind's default `--tw-ring-offset-color` is a literal white, a leak in all but name; the design gate now proves the utility emits `var(--background)` by compiling it, not by grepping for the class.
- [10-06]: DS-05, DS-08 and DS-09 stay Pending after the plan that defines them, because each requirement's wording contains an ADOPTION clause (`applies app-wide`, `the 19 literal recipes are replaced`, `is the standard for booker-facing primary actions`) owned by 10-07 through 10-09. Delivering a contract is not delivering its adoption.
- [10-06]: The grep-versus-comment collision is now a FIVE-time pattern (10-01, 10-03, 10-04, 10-05, 10-06 — five banned literals in one file this time). Same resolution every time: keep the reasoning, name the literal descriptively, and attach the measured number so the prose says strictly more than the literal would have.
- [10-06]: Negative assertions are observed RED before being trusted. Both of this plan's guards were reinstated as failures (alpha ring, alpha brand hover) and each turned exactly one test red — a negative that has never been seen to fail is indistinguishable from a typo'd matcher.
- [10-04]: Tailwind v4 emits a utility only when its content scan finds the class name, and NOTHING in `src/` says `text-display` or `shadow-overlay` yet — the call-site migrations are later plans. So a compiled-CSS assertion on a newly declared `@theme inline` step is unreachable through the plain `compileGlobalsCss()`. Added `compileGlobalsCssWith(utilities)`, which appends Tailwind's own `@source inline(…)` safelist at TEST time rather than shipping a safelist in `globals.css`: the stylesheet stays the token contract with no build directive in it, and the forcing is visible at the assertion that needs it. Each such block carries a positive control (`text-figure`, `shadow-floating`) proving the safelist cannot fabricate a step whose `@theme` key was never declared.
- [10-04]: Tailwind's automatic source detection scans `.planning/**/*.md`, so PROSE emits utilities — the compiled bundle carries `.bg-zinc-50` (a leak-gate fixture example in 10-RESEARCH.md) and `.outline-ring\/50` (the anti-pattern this plan deletes), neither of which appears anywhere in `src/`. Pre-existing and out of this plan's scope, so logged to the phase's `deferred-items.md` and flagged to 10-12: any assertion of the form "utility X exists in the compiled output" can be satisfied by a sentence in a markdown file.
- [10-04]: `--font-weight-medium` is deliberately aliased onto each theme's emphasis weight rather than kept as a third step, and the resulting 500→600 shift in court is an ACCEPTED visible change on the same footing as the accent deepening. Two weights per theme is the contract; the alias is what lets the 70 shipped `font-medium` call sites need zero edits. Asserted in `type-scale.test.ts` so a future reader cannot "fix" it back to 500 silently.
- [10-04]: The global z-index and motion tokens went into a NEW plain `:root { … }` rule placed after both theme blocks, closing 10-03's deferred `readGlobalTokens()` throw. Declaring them in a theme block would either break THEME-02's key-set equality (one theme only) or claim they travel (both themes) — and a per-theme z-index lets one theme reorder the app's layers. `elevation-z.test.ts` asserts the four z names appear in NEITHER theme block, so the D-05 split stays honest as later plans add tokens.
- [10-04]: `requirements.mark-complete` run for DS-04 only, of the plan's five claimed IDs. DS-02, DS-03 and DS-05 each have a second half this plan does not own (the arbitrary `text-[NNpx]` migration, the 14 shadow call sites, and the 13 `focus-visible:ring-ring/50` sites), and THEME-02 needs the next-themes runtime. Same resolution as 10-01/10-02/10-03: a premature `Complete` is invisible for the rest of the phase.
- [10-01]: Package legitimacy gate resolved by the developer — `culori@4.0.2` **Approved** (slopcheck `[OK]`, MIT, `github.com/Evercoder/culori`, no install-time script for registry consumers; named by D-12) and `@types/culori@4.0.1` **Approved** after its `[ASSUMED]` gap was closed by confirming `npm view culori types` returns empty. Nothing was installed before the gate resolved.
- [10-01]: `vitest.design.config.ts` declares NO `globalSetup` and NO `setupFiles` — those two keys are exactly what makes `vitest.config.ts` require Docker. Adding either to the design config silently reintroduces the database dependency, which is why the header comment says so in the file itself.
- [10-01]: The `.tsx` collection trap is closed on BOTH halves — the design config includes `.test.ts` + `.test.tsx`, AND `vitest.config.ts` excludes `tests/design/**`. Verified by measurement (`vitest list --filesOnly`), not by intent: the main config collects 0 files under `tests/design`.
- [10-01]: "Runs with no database" was PROVEN, not asserted — `docker stop fitout-db-1`, gate green in 841ms, main config exit 1 with `cannot reach the test database` as the control, container restarted. A DB-free claim verified while the DB is up is not a verification.
- [10-01]: Task 2's literal `grep -c "globalSetup\|setupFiles" == 0` criterion conflicts with the same task's mandated header comment. Honoured the comment (it is what stops a future contributor re-adding the keys) and verified the intent instead: zero occurrences on any non-comment line.
- [10-01]: `requirements.mark-complete DS-06 DS-13` deliberately NOT run — both IDs are also claimed by plans 10-02/03/14/17, and this plan ships the harness, not the contrast or leak tests. Ticking them now would put a false `Complete` in the traceability table for the rest of the phase.
- [09-15]: An e2e fork test seeds a CONTROL of the other mode. "X is absent" is not a finding; "X is absent HERE and present THERE, same host, same day" is. Without it, case 1's three absences would also pass on a build that rendered no availability picker at all.
- [09-15]: Prove a copy/layout absence by scanning the rendered document's own `innerText` for the forbidden SHAPE and whitelisting the allowed framing by count — not by `not.toBeVisible()` on a guessed locator. On this page both "hours" and a clock range are legitimately present (the cancellation disclosure and the venue's opening line), so a literal-absence gate is either always red or quietly vacuous.
- [09-15]: A plan step that is physically unreachable gets SPLIT into the states that exist, not forced. A fully booked date is programmatically `disabled`, so its day panel cannot be clicked open — the sold-out copy is reached through the OC-13 advisory-picker path and the disabled cell is asserted after a reload.

- [08-09]: A mutation gate must name the ARTIFACT it mutates. Mutating a test's inlined copy of production SQL proves the *pattern*; mutating the *production module* proves the *shipped code*. These are different claims and only the second is a regression guard. The GROUP-05 gate does the first only — recorded as such rather than reported as a clean pass.
- [08-09]: Both mutations were run and both outcomes recorded, instead of silently substituting the one that works for the one the plan named. The divergence between them IS the finding.
- [08-09]: The production-lock coverage gap was LOGGED, not closed — closing it is new test authorship, outside a plan declaring `<files></files>` and "no code changes". It is `deferred-items.md` item 4.
- [08-09]: An optional verification step that could not be exercised is recorded as NOT-EXERCISED with its reason (NULL `extra_head_fee`), never quietly dropped and never counted as verified. An untested surface described as verified is worse than one described as untested.
- [08-09]: A blocking checkpoint's completed-task evidence is parked in a `{phase}-{plan}-PROGRESS.md` so a long human pause cannot force a re-run of an expensive mutation; the PROGRESS file is folded into the SUMMARY and deleted on resume, so no stale `status: in-progress` artifact survives.
- [Roadmap]: 8-phase dependency-driven order — identity → supply → availability + double-booking guarantee → booking core + search (no payment) → payments → full instant/request integration → bookings management + cancellation → group bookings. Ordering is non-negotiable for correctness.
- [Roadmap]: Double-booking prevented at the DB level (Postgres GiST exclusion constraint on tstzrange, scoped by listing) — must exist before any booking insert (Phase 3).
- [Roadmap]: Bookability gate built in Phase 2 (gate listing bookability, not creation, on payout-readiness) so it can never be bypassed later — now via PayMongo `merchant.activated`/`activation_status: activated` (D-20); previously Stripe Connect `payouts_enabled`.
- [Quick 260709-id2]: Payments provider switched Stripe → **PayMongo** for the Philippines launch (D-20, supersedes D-17/D-19) — QRPh + GCash + Maya + cards; hold-until-session via on-demand `inhouse` wallet-to-wallet transfers (NOT payment-splitting); bookability gate = `merchant.activated`. Validated by a live sandbox spike (auth/QRPh-accept/card-void confirmed; card manual-capture is a gated advanced feature; QRPh refunds supported per docs). Follow-ups: re-plan 02-06; mark D-17/D-19 superseded in 02-CONTEXT.md.
